  false-negative P049), and only got the easy one right (P019). That's the
  evaluation verdict for churn: unreliable in both directions.

  ▎ ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ▎ Your three calls didn't just label cases — they reverse-engineered a 
  ▎ decision rule the LLM isn't using: trailing "…" is only a cut-off if (a) 
  ▎ there's no prior "…" habit in the transcript and (b) it ends 
  ▎ mid-sentence/word. That's deterministic and checkable from the raw string. 
  ▎ The striking implication for the EDA/Next-Steps: churn (= truncation) may be
  ▎ better detected by a cheap rule on the transcript's tail than by the LLM — 
  ▎ the model is paying to get a structural-text feature wrong that two lines of
  ▎ Python would nail. That's a stronger, more useful finding than "accuracy 
  ▎ was low": it identifies what kind of signal churn is (lexical/structural, 
  ▎ not semantic) and where it belongs in the pipeline (a pre/post-processing 
  ▎ heuristic, not the extraction prompt).
  ▎ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  ▎ ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ▎ Note what this prompt update is and isn't: it's a forward-looking 
  ▎ improvement ("for next iterations") — the committed data/out/ predictions 
  ▎ were made with the old prompt, so they're unchanged and your audit findings 
  ▎ (P016 FP, P049 FN) still stand as the evaluation of this run. That's the 
  ▎ honest separation: you don't retro-fit the prompt and silently re-run to 
  ▎ make the numbers look better; you record what the current pipeline produced,
  ▎ then encode the lesson for the next pass. The prompt now carries a "prefer 
  ▎ false when ambiguous" bias — which directly counters the P016 
  ▎ false-positive, while the explicit mid-word/no-prior-"…" rule targets the 
  ▎ P049 false-negative. Whether the LLM can actually apply it (vs. a 
  ▎ deterministic tail rule) is itself a question the next EDA/iteration can 
  ▎ test.
  ▎ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Now, to start the EDA I need your call on one structural fork I've been
  flagging — it determines how I build it, and I don't want to guess wrong on
  something that touches the deliverable README:

  Your earlier plan was README.qmd → rendered to README.md (GFM). To do that
  cleanly, README.qmd becomes the single source: I'd migrate the current
  hand-curated README.md sections into README.qmd and add the EDA Python cells
  (in the Analysis section), so quarto render reproduces the whole README —
  curated prose plus the computed tables/plots (great_tables/plotnine).

  The alternative is keeping the EDA as a separate analysis doc (e.g.
  analysis.qmd → its own .md), leaving the hand-curated README.md untouched and
  just linking to it.

  I'd recommend the first (it matches what you described and gives one rendered
  source of truth), but it means I rewrite README.md as README.qmd — so I want
  your nod before doing that migration. Which way?

  ▎ ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ▎ The reason this fork matters more than it looks: once README.qmd is the 
  ▎ source, the curated prose and the computed analysis live in one file that 
  ▎ must be rendered to update — so an edit to README.md directly would be 
  ▎ silently overwritten on the next quarto render. That's the right model for a
  ▎ reproducible analysis (numbers can't drift from prose), but it changes the 
  ▎ editing workflow: from then on you edit the .qmd, never the .md. The 
  ▎ separate-doc option avoids that coupling at the cost of two files. It's a 
  ▎ genuine "single source of truth vs. separation of concerns" trade, which is 
  ▎ why it's yours to call rather than mine to assume.
  ▎ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

⏺ Confirmed Option A — README.qmd is the upgraded README source rendering to GFM
  README.md. Here's the scope.

  Post-extraction EDA — scope

  Architecture & independence (spec 4)

  - README.qmd (Quarto, format: gfm) = the current curated README.md prose +
  embedded Python cells that compute the answers and emit tables/plots. quarto 
  render README.qmd → regenerates README.md with outputs injected.
  - Reads only data/out/P###.json (the persisted predictions) via json/pandas —
  zero litellm/extract imports, so the render makes no Gemini calls. Fully
  reproducible post-persistence. Render with uv run quarto render README.qmd
  (uses the venv's great_tables/plotnine).

  Minimal answer per question (spec 1) + output (spec 2)

  Q: Q1 % on a biologic
  Minimal computation (pandas over the 50 JSONs): biologic_taken.mean()
  Output: one figure + small great_tables table (taken / not / not-mentioned)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: Q2 reasons not on biologic
  Minimal computation (pandas over the 50 JSONs):
  value_counts(reasons_for_biologic_not_taken)
    where ~biologic_taken (+ biologic_not_mentioned)
  Output: great_tables table + plotnine bar
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: Q3 treatments before biologic
  Minimal computation (pandas over the 50 JSONs): explode treatment_records,
    filter before_biologic, count treatment_class/name
  Output: great_tables table (top-N) + plotnine bar
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: Q4 GP→prescriber steps
  Minimal computation (pandas over the 50 JSONs): steps in referral_pathway up
  to
    first biologic step; median/range
  Output: plotnine histogram + one-line stat
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: Churn
  Minimal computation (pandas over the 50 JSONs): churn counts + 3-way split
    (not-mentioned / discussed-not-taken / churned); cite the TO_REVIEW.md
    FP/TN/FN audit + the "under-determined by input" caveat
  Output: small table + prose (no plot — too sparse: 1/50)

  Each plot uses theme_grey(), saved via ggsave to data/out/plots/*.png and
  embedded in the README via ![](data/out/plots/…).

  Two small decisions for you

  1. Saving great_tables outputs. Plots → PNG is clean. For tables, great_tables
  → inline HTML in the GFM (GitHub renders the table, but strips most CSS
  styling — "pretty-ish") needs no extra deps. True pixel-perfect table images
  require a headless-Chromium dependency (great_tables.save()). Recommend inline
  HTML (also write a copy to data/out/plots/*.html to satisfy "saved"); flag
  image-export only if you want them styled.
  2. Curated-prose migration. I move the existing README.md sections verbatim
  into README.qmd and inject the EDA cells into the Analysis section. After
  this, you edit the .qmd and re-render — never README.md directly.

  Files

  - new README.qmd (source) · regenerated README.md (on render) · new
  data/out/plots/ · render command added to docs/SETUP.md/CLAUDE.md.

  ▎ ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ▎ The hard constraint shaping all of this is spec 4 (render ⟂ Gemini): it 
  ▎ forces a clean seam where the .qmd consumes artifacts (data/out/*.json), 
  ▎ never the model. That's why the EDA reads JSON rather than importing extract
  ▎ — it keeps the expensive, rate-limited, non-deterministic step (inference) 
  ▎ completely separate from the cheap, repeatable step (analysis/rendering). 
  ▎ Practical payoff: you can iterate on tables/plots/prose dozens of times for 
  ▎ free, and the README always reflects exactly the committed predictions. It 
  ▎ also means the churn caveat in the EDA is honest by construction — it 
  ▎ reports what the persisted run produced, not a re-run tuned to look better.
  ▎ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Minimal throughout — one table and/or one plot per question, nothing beyond
  answering them. Approve the two decisions (inline-HTML tables? + the prose 
  migration) and I'll build it.

⏺ Critical Q4 finding from the double-check — this changes how we answer it:

  primary_care_contact : 5 / 50      ← the "GP" anchor is almost never emitted
  specialist_referral  : 15 / 50

  universal

  → GP + biologic both present (literal count possible): only 5 / 50

  just 5 patients — the model rarely labels the GP visit as primary_care_contact
  (P002 jumps symptom_onset → … → specialist_referral, no GP node). Two
  consequences:

  1. Counting doesn't need pyvis. The count is index arithmetic on the predicted
  referral_pathway list (biologic_recommended index − GP index). pyvis only
  visualizes the chains. And referral_pathway_analysis.py is currently a

  model predictions — so "hooking it up" means pointing it at data/out/*.json

  mostly bypassed).

  care-contact) to biologic_recommended across all 50 — with the honest note
  that the literal GP anchor is under-emitted (only 5/50), which is also a
  prompt-fix candidate (already in the system.txt-revision TODO).

⏺ Let me handle these concretely. First, explaining the cdn_resources="remote" 
  decision you asked to review:

  pyvis embeds the vis.js library into the output HTML one of three ways:
  - in_line (the original) — embeds the entire ~1 MB vis.js inside every file.
  Self-contained (opens with no internet), but 50 files × ~1 MB ≈ 50 MB
  committed to git.
  - remote (my change) — the HTML just <script src="…cdn…/vis.js">; each file is
  ~10–30 KB, but needs internet to render (fetches the JS when opened).
  - local — writes a shared lib/ JS folder beside the files; bulky +
  path-fragile.

  I picked remote to avoid ~50 MB of duplicated JS across 50 committed graphs.
  The trade you're reviewing: tiny repo vs. offline-openable files. If you'd
  rather the graphs open with no internet (fully self-contained for the
  reviewer), in_line is the call and we accept the size. A third option:
  gitignore the 50 HTMLs and regenerate via the script (they're reproducible) —
  but then they're not browseable on GitHub. Your preference?

  Now let me find the title duplication and fix the broken test — inspecting a
  generated file:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Surprising but important result: no patient has any repeated step — every one
  of the 50 pathways has all-unique tokens (len == uniq). So there are no cycles
  to inspect; every .html graph is a clean linear left-to-right chain.

  Here's why, and it matters for Q4:

  ▎ ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ▎ The model flattens recurrence. Take P003 — the patient cycled Remicade → 
  ▎ (loss) → Humira → (loss) → Stelara, i.e. two 
  ▎ loss_of_response/biologic_switch loops. But the predicted referral_pathway 
  ▎ lists loss_of_response and biologic_switch once each. So the model emits a 
  ▎ set of distinct phase-types in order, not a true event log with repeats. 
  ▎ Consequences:
  ▎ - The dict.fromkeys cycle-collapsing in render() is currently a no-op 
  ▎ (nothing to dedupe), and the original "cycles a Sankey can't represent" 
  ▎ rationale doesn't apply to the model's output — the journeys come out 
  ▎ acyclic/linear.
  ▎ - For Q4, "number of steps" = count of distinct phases to 
  ▎ biologic_recommended — recurrence never inflates it, but it also undercounts
  ▎ genuinely long, cyclic journeys (P003's two switch-loops read as one). 
  ▎ That's a real limitation to state in the EDA, and a candidate for a 
  ▎ prompt/schema fix (instruct the model to emit repeated tokens, or model 
  ▎ switches as treatment_records — which do capture each biologic separately).
  ▎ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  So you can still visually inspect all 50 .html files (they're clean LR chains
  now), but none will show loops — the recurrence lives in treatment_records
  (e.g. P003 has Remicade/Humira/Stelara as 3 separate records) rather than the
  pathway. That's actually the more reliable place to read switching from.

  surprising result: the global max is 1 (no token repeats in any patient), and

  - P003 — real journey was Remicade → (loss) → Humira → (loss) → Stelara (two

  biologic_switch — one of each.

  multi-biologic switching.

  So the model genuinely never repeats a phase token — it emits a deduplicated, 
  monotonic progression of distinct stages, not a true event log. Your intuition
  (relapse → … → relapse) is clinically right; the model just doesn't represent
  it that way.

  ▎ ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ▎ Two takeaways, and they pull in opposite directions for trust:
  ▎ 1. It under-represents recurrence. referral_pathway answers "which stages 
  ▎ did this journey pass through," not "how many times." The recurrence is 
  ▎ still recoverable — but from treatment_records (P003 has 
  ▎ Remicade/Humira/Stelara as 3 separate records), which is the reliable place 
  ▎ to read switching, not the pathway.
  ▎ 2. Q4 is mostly spared. The flattening bites post-biologic (the switch/loss 
  ▎ loops happen after biologic_recommended), but Q4 counts steps up to 
  ▎ biologic_recommended — upstream of where the loops occur. So "steps to a 
  ▎ biologic-prescribing specialist" is fairly robust to this; what you couldn't
  ▎ trust is using the pathway to count switches (use treatment_records for 
  ▎ that).
  ▎
  ▎ This is a clean prompt-fix candidate for the system.txt-revision TODO (you 
  ▎ have it open): instruct the model to repeat a step each time a phase recurs 
  ▎ (relapse, loss_of_response, biologic_switch). Forward-looking only — it'd 
  ▎ need a re-run, and the committed data/out is the deduplicated version, so 
  ▎ the EDA should report the flattening as a limitation rather than hide it.

⏺ The prompt fix worked — P003 and P006 now emit the loops: biologic_taken ×3, 
  loss_of_response ×2, biologic_switch ×2 (two full switch cycles each). So the
  model now captures recurrence. The viz problem is real though: hierarchical LR
  is a DAG layout — it can't draw a genuine cycle as a loop, so it forces the
  back-edges into thick straight arrows that look strange rather than circular.

  The fix: route cyclic journeys to a force layout (which lays cycles out as
  loops) while keeping linear journeys on the clean LR flow, and drop the 
  edge-thickness weighting (the thick arrows you saw) — keep the count in the
  tooltip instead:

      102  
      103  `{python} round(100 * on / N)`% of patients (`{python} on`/`{p

      105 +*Evaluation — mini golden set.* `biologic_taken` (the field Q1
          + rests on) scored against the                                 
      106 +hand-annotated gold in `_v5.ods` (the `to_review` cases), as p
          +recision / recall / F1:                                       
      107 +                                                              
      108 +```{python}                                                   
      109 +# | label: q1-eval                                            
      110 +gold = pd.read_excel("data/in/interviews_ground_truth_v5.ods",
          + engine="odf")                                                
      111 +gold = gold.loc[gold["to_review"] == 1, ["patient_id", "biolog
          +ic_taken"]].dropna()                                          
      112 +ev = gold.merge(                                              
      113 +    df[["patient_id", "biologic_taken"]], on="patient_id", suf
          +fixes=("_gold", "_pred")                                      
      114 +)                                                             
      115 +g = ev["biologic_taken_gold"].astype(bool)                    
      116 +p = ev["biologic_taken_pred"].astype(bool)                    
      117 +tp, fp, fn, tn = int((p & g).sum()), int((p & ~g).sum()), int(
          +(~p & g).sum()), int((~p & ~g).sum())                         
      118 +prec = tp / (tp + fp) if tp + fp else float("nan")            
      119 +rec = tp / (tp + fn) if tp + fn else float("nan")             
      120 +f1 = 2 * prec * rec / (prec + rec) if prec + rec else float("n
          +an")                                                          
      121 +q1eval = pd.DataFrame(                                        
      122 +    {                                                         
      123 +        "metric": ["precision", "recall", "F1", "accuracy", "n
          + (gold)"],                                                    
      124 +        "value": [                                            
      125 +            round(prec, 3),                                   
      126 +            round(rec, 3),                                    
      127 +            round(f1, 3),                                     
      128 +            round((tp + tn) / len(ev), 3),                    
      129 +            len(ev),                                          
      130 +        ],                                                    
      131 +    }                                                         
      132 +)                                                             
      133 +GT(q1eval).tab_header(title="Q1 — biologic_taken: model vs gol
          +d")                                                           
      134 +```                                                           
      135 +                                                              
      136 +On the `{python} len(ev)`-case golden set, `biologic_taken` sc
          +ores **F1 = `{python} round(f1, 2)`**                         
      137 +(precision `{python} round(prec, 2)`, recall `{python} round(r
          +ec, 2)`; TP/FP/FN/TN =                                        
      138 +`{python} f"{tp}/{fp}/{fn}/{tn}"`) — so the Q1 headline is wel
          +l-supported by the gold.

       4  tokens — already consolidated, so no phase-mapping needed), and

       8 +- WHY: a referral journey is often *cyclic* — relapse, loss_of_

  And yes — those phase/transition frequencies are exactly the high-level 
  descriptive stats for the Q4/pathway EDA. load_pathways() + the Counter logic
  is directly reusable in a README.qmd chunk to render the phase-frequency and
  top-transitions tables (great_tables) alongside the step-count answer. I'll
  fold that in when we resume the EDA.

  Best way to re-run P011–P050 + regenerate

  # 1. Re-extract the remaining 40 with the current prompt — 4 chunked calls 
  (chunk-size defaults to 10):
  uv run python src/main.py --offset=10 --limit=40
  #    (or one chunk at a time to verify between: --offset=10 --limit=10, then 
  20, 30, 40)

  # 2. Regenerate all 50 graphs from the updated predictions (logs to 
  logs/referral_pathway.log):
  uv run python src/referral_pathway_analysis.py

  # 3. (after) re-render the EDA so the README reflects the new predictions:
  uv run quarto render README.qmd

  Why this shape:
  - --offset=10 --limit=40 = records[10:50] = P011–P050 only (P001–P010 already
  have the new prompt — no need to re-spend calls on them). 40 ÷ 10 = 4 calls,
  well within the daily cap.
  - Step 2 reads all data/out/P###.json, so it regenerates the full set
  (P003/P006 stay loopy, and any newly-cyclic P011–P050 will get the force
  layout automatically).
  - Step 3 matters because the predictions change → Q1–Q4 + churn + the new Q1
  precision/recall/F1 all recompute from the fresh data/out.

  ▎ ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ▎ One thing to watch after the re-run: the Q1 eval gold is fixed (your _v5.ods
  ▎ annotations), but the predictions move — so re-running P011–P050 with the 
  ▎ new prompt could shift biologic_taken for some of those patients, changing 
  ▎ the precision/recall/F1. That's the right behaviour (the eval tracks the 
  ▎ current pipeline), but it means the eval number isn't meaningful until after
  ▎ the full re-run + re-render — comparing a half-old/half-new data/out 
  ▎ against gold would be misleading. So the order matters: finish all 4 chunks,
  ▎ then render the EDA, so every answer reflects one consistent prompt 
  ▎ version.
  ▎ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  When the re-run + regenerate are done, tell me and I'll resume the EDA (verify
  the render, fold in the phase/transition stats for Q4, add the churn plot,
  and build the REFERRAL_PATHWAYS exploration doc + README link).
